# Shared project context

Last substantive handoff: **November 15, 2026, America/Los_Angeles**. Read this with [owner requirements](OWNER_REQUIREMENTS.md) and [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md). Later verified changes should update this file, not create a competing project.

**Current implementation:** New Crystal Run players start with two Laser Miners, Inkbeam, Needler, Ballcaster, Vacuum, Compactor and two Emptiers. The seven non-Emptiers are permanent. Returning profiles keep earned drones and prior fleets, with a targeted correction for the short-lived 17-drone starter grant. The backpack starts with ten slots. Each ordinary crystal gives one raw rock. Across Crystal Run, larger crystals take longer to cut, fracture in more stages and sell for more per slot. Needler fires quickly and hits exposed deep cores 25% harder. Ballcaster builds a heavier shot and hits the first two layers of deep crystals 50% harder. Field hardness still triples by depth. Emptiers collect loose ore directly and sell it one item per trip. The Compactor packs matching rocks into one block worth their total value. The first pack offers Double Beam, Loose Seams and Aftershock. Prestige grants one of three stable permanent drone choices and an opening +50% base mining power per prestige (first three), then +25 percentage points. The first three sale goals are now 160, 400 and 1,000 Quartz; later thresholds retain their previous values.

## November 15 — ground-only selection and staggered reinforcements

The two-click selection still uses an expanding ground preview, rock tints, a confirmation wash, and a persistent full footprint. The custom cursor, selection walls and edge particles were removed. Selection raycasts run on click.

Workers take unclaimed rocks first, in order of least remaining health. Once a worker runs out of unclaimed choices, it waits 2.15–2.87 seconds before joining a same-crew cut. Shares favor the rock with the fewest assignments and then the lowest remaining health. A rock has up to ten stations. Newly ready rocks take priority for available workers, and existing shared cuts continue through regrowth. Different crews keep separate claims.

Shared **DRONEREVOLUTION** was already open and remains in Edit, place **124078027733625**, universe **10774898335**. The three Script Sync roots reported `Errored`; a guarded Script Editor install updated the two controllers after verifying all seven baseline Sources against `9337fc6`. The next shared Studio Play loads ground-only selection and staggered reinforcements. **No Roblox publication.**

The native Edit component check verified the two-click flow, ground-only markers, rock tint, wash and recall cleanup. The production-server fixture with API doubles spread ten miners across three high-health rocks in crews of four, three and three, verified staggered starts, mixed laser and gun contacts, foreign claim exclusion, regrowth and the ten distinct stations on a final rock. The mining-flight and beam suites passed. No Play session was started. Visual spacing during flight and multiplayer remain unverified. Reproduce with `tools/studio_selector_distance_checks.lua`, `python3 tools/test_field_pickup_flow.py`, `python3 tools/script_sync.py` and `python3 tools/build.py --previous-commit 9337fc6`.

## November 15 — field preview, weak-first mining and final-rock teamwork

The plus eases between two-stud grid cells. The first click expands a ground preview and tints the rocks as the footprint reaches them. Click inside the preview to confirm it or click elsewhere on a valid bed to move it. The accepted footprint keeps its full base fill. Confirmation opens the walls, sends sparks around the edges, and briefly brightens the selected rocks over the fading wash.

In the selected area, each drone chooses the unclaimed rock with the least remaining health. Up to five same-crew workers can join the sole standing rock. Foreign claims remain exclusive. Growing replacements count as standing rocks, so extra workers release the shared target and spread out as the new rock becomes ready. Existing role abilities and ore payouts still resolve on the server.

## November 15 — winding paths and landmark outcrops

The owner's Play screenshot of `428512d` showed the deposits reading as rows. Each Crystal Run bed now has a separately placed set of uneven clusters and its own bent side trail. The main walkway shifts laterally across the beds, with entrances aligned at the seams. Two tall spires and two wide boulders anchor each bed. They regrow at their original sites with the same shapes, and mining drones keep at least 4.5 studs over their intact upper bounds. The bed limit is now 64 instead of 78 to leave more ground open between groups. Work, ore value, and regrowth timing still follow the existing crystal rules.

## November 15 — Voronoi rock clusters and connected paths

Crystal Run rocks favor eight deposit centers per bed. The Voronoi boundary between neighboring deposits creates narrow side paths that meet a wider, bent crosswalk and a straight route through all five beds. New growth tries the emptied spot first when it still fits. Tilt-aware clearance protects the routes and neighboring rocks throughout growth and staged cuts. Upper-end rolls now produce a mix of taller spires and broader outcrops using the existing mineral cuts. Mining work, yields, population limits and regrowth timing use the existing rules.

## November 14 — field aiming, pickup priority, and wall feedback

Clicks trace past non-solid clutter and can use a reachable rock's center when a shallow ray overshoots. The server still checks bed bounds, range, and combat state and now replies to placement commands. Reachable, settled loose ore goes to the player's backpack even when a courier has started its approach, provided there is room and a clear path. The server still grants each pickup only once. Courier grabs already made and ore actively entering the Compactor keep their normal handling.

The cursor draws a thin outline. The accepted pin alone keeps its walls. A click flashes its floor and the rocks in the same footprint white. Active mining outlines can coexist with the flash and have no game-side distance cutoff or ten-rock cap. A new wall rises quickly, holds its bright flash, then fades linearly to a visible minimum. The perimeter sparks live up to 1.24 seconds. Reduced settings still dampen flashes or disable wall motion and sparks.

## November 14 — calm formation and heavier flight after rejected swarm

The owner rejected `d3320e8` as frantic at rest and in flight. Removed time-driven looping/wandering goals, low-speed tangential avoidance and repeated velocity correction per simulation substep. ## November 14 — physical drone swarm and rock contact

The crew now forms a loose swarm when its owner moves, with independently seeded height, speed and drift. It settles back into a ring after the owner stops. Neighbors exert separation and passing forces, with light alignment during escort flight. The server integrates thrust, drag and mass; observers follow the same flight inputs and bank into acceleration. Precision work keeps its station, and Nomad retains its high flight pattern. A single backward impulse moves each gun's actual hull at release. Ballcaster kicks harder than Needler, and a valid launched shot can finish while the hull corrects. A one-ore load gives a courier more inertia, a 21% lower maximum flight speed and a slower lift. Mature field cuts block avatars and stop colliding on break. Regrowth waits for space before becoming solid; ore pickup and mining keep their prior soft obstruction rule for these cuts.

## November 14 — compact finish correction; polygon Diamond rejected

Implementation/native revision **f183709**. The owner rejected the preceding compact finishes and Diamond with actual Play screenshots. Compact descriptions now inherit their original mineral's material, opacity, vertex alpha, texture, color function and inclusion count/colors/sizes. Inclusion positions are remapped into the compact body. Ruby returns from accidental .12 to .72 opacity with 30 white grains; Violet returns to .76 and its original grains; compact Quartz restores its texture and blue/gold grains. ## November 11 — refine pack timing, crew selection and work effects

The crate hold now follows a continuous curve with a quick push to 57% by 0.62 seconds, a slower shoulder to 75% by 1.32 seconds, and a long tail from 92% at 3.25 seconds to Legendary release at 6.85 seconds. Those progress values describe Legendary. Each earlier rarity normalizes the same curve to its own stop. The interpolation matches velocity across the sampled points, including when the bar changes color. The sample points approximate the visible sweep in the reference. [Devilish Mortis opening footage](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CsS2OfinXbg&t=3s)

Three reels run in 3D across the full viewport. Thick card stock and a bevel make the passing cards read in profile. Settled cards keep a single art preview, rarity-colored stock, and stable screen text. The renderer uses viewport geometry for the reels and settled stock to remove the previous coplanar SurfaceGui and per-frame canvas cropping. The crew camera looks down from about 59 degrees. Cycling leaves the ring fixed and lifts the selected drone four studs. Ineligible drones are gray and 50% transparent.

The starter laser again pulls particles into its emitter, fires the original color pop, and leaves its expanding planar ring and drifting dust. Its original beam width, contact position and damage cadence still govern the cut. Needler shoots a thin tracer with sharp sparks. Ballcaster fires a bright core with a broader wake and a compact pressure ring at confirmed contact. The Compactor pulls docked inputs into the closing jaws, holds briefly, and ejects the block 0.82 seconds after the press starts. This incorporates the pending Compactor changes that finish a loaded batch at its old dock on a distant repin and release a single input safely.

The pack timing test checked continuous bar velocity at the curve knots. Beam, workshop, Crystal Run, pickup and isolated hardware checks passed with API or storage doubles. The projectile contact fixture confirmed that the head disappears on contact and its wake finishes independently. The broader gadget and logistics suites still hit the client-inertia fixture and schema-six migration failures reproduced on `bce4467`. Detached Studio Edit checks constructed the beveled card and verified fixed crew slots, a four-stud lift and 50% transparency. Live animation and multiplayer remain unverified. ## November 11 — Reclaim extends deep cuts

Reclaim restores six battery per stack on the final break and on a hit that reaches a new fracture in Violet or Emerald. A hit that crosses several layers pays once. Regrowth does not restore charge to previously fractured layers. An Aftershock finish recharges the ally already working the target when there is one. The card and Workshop describe the expanded effect, using the existing card layout and green battery cue.

In matched server-controller runs, one laser miner fitted with Double Beam and Reclaim cut six legal Emerald rock sizes. ## Latest follow-up — fitted upgrades and card slots

A fresh Crystal Run's first 20-Quartz pack has three parts. Double Beam fits two cyan emitters to a chosen permanent laser miner and doubles its laser damage against its current crystal. The existing playtest showed three specialists on their small replicated proxies. The client renderer had used the purchase list as its set of known drone types. It now reads all role definitions and creates the visible local models for all five test drones. The dart thrower, ballcaster, vacuum and planter render at twice their former linear scale. The original miner renders 22% larger. Their panels use broader role colors, light trim and dark working hardware. The revised surfaces and silhouettes follow the readability approach described by [Valve](https://cdn.steamstatic.com/apps/valve/2007/NPAR07_IllustrativeRenderingInTeamFortress2.pdf) and [Riot](https://playvalorant.com/en-us/news/dev/the-art-of-valorant-map-environments/).

Projectile receipts drive local launch hardware and carry the firing drone's ID so the shot can originate at the visible muzzle. The client also animates the vacuum intake and seed heads while those drones work. In the shared Studio place, all five proxies were hidden by their local views, both launchers and the planter moved, and the vacuum intake extended 0.31 studs while ore reached the backpack. The actual starter field was inspected in the desktop viewport. The workshop, flight and focused hardware tests passed. The broad specialist suite stopped at a schema-six fixture that also fails on `688790b`. Mobile readability and multiplayer remain unverified.

## Latest follow-up — drone production loop

The first pack lets laser cracks echo into one eligible neighboring crystal, refunds six battery per break, or lets more of the same ore arrive early from farther away. Later packs still offer three saved choices and increasing stack prices. Field Charger lets depleted workers recharge at an active pin. The Workshop Automation tab offers a Vacuum Drone for two Stars, a Crystal Planter for three and Auto Sell for six. The vacuum pulls loose ore through its intake toward the owner's backpack and stops gathering when the bag is full. The planter consumes an actual vacancy at the pin. A full bag or ore left out of reach appears in the pin status. Loose ore can keep accumulating while the bag is full.

The five-drone Studio crew replaces the active test roster and preserves the three permanent starter records. The dart and energy ball fly to crystals from the side and apply damage on arrival. The 100-stud horizontal leash clears the pin and its reservations and brings the crew along. Players still see one local pickup count beside the backpack, grouped from authoritative receipts. Dropped and carried tools are disabled in the Crystal Run game. Old saved equipment records remain valid.

The workshop, mining-flight and pickup checks passed with API doubles. Long-session pacing, player enjoyment and multiplayer remain unverified.

## Latest follow-up — reference shading and no world outlines

The enabled Mining Away server computes shade from the remaining health divided by the current size band, minus a small offset for the band. DRONEREVOLUTION uses that formula. At the crack it uses the outgoing band, then switches to the new band on the next hit. The former timed black flash was removed. In the native Edit probe the first crack changed the width from 3.135 to 2.351 studs and the normalized red channel rose from 0.749 at the crack to 0.922 on the following hit.

## Earlier follow-up — persistent tool marks and health-based size

A resource keeps up to eight distinct impact or heat marks for its lifetime. Close hits of the same kind deepen the existing mark without erasing other marks. At capacity the earlier marks stay. The server replicates the full history so another observer can reconstruct the same solid surface. Marks stay attached as a resource shrinks.

Junk and ore now use the same health-stage size rule for drone and tool damage. Sword slices and melt still change the shape of the top. Server and client use the same sizing equation and keep the resource grounded.

Verified in shared Edit at place 124078027733625, universe 10774898335, loaded version 76. An Edit fixture measured the same depth for the original dent after a second dent was added. Both marks survived resizing and reconstruction on a fresh view with the six photo decals. Removed the fixtures. Field-tool, rock-damage, core-loop and consumable tests passed, including equal-size comparisons and heat marks. No new Play or multiplayer session. No Roblox publication.

Script Sync delivered both controllers. All seven Source hashes and all three roots matched the canonical snapshot. The Source-only refresh preserved the island map and the backup scripts. Reproduce with `python3 tools/build.py --previous-commit 3982df59e023f32614a09793eab1877cfd94ed87`.

## Latest follow-up — falling junk in Quartz

Click the Quartz bed or a landed prop to order the crew. The starter miner needs no card. Up to four props fall per wave, with replenishment at two or fewer unclaimed objects and a cap of ten live objects including those in flight. The server chooses clear, supported landing spots and assigns single-object claims before flight. Crews without a job go first, with least-recently-served ordering within each active job count and up to two simultaneous jobs per crew. Recall, leaving the clearing, loss of the worker and disconnect release its claims.

The fall rotation includes the original pip, gingham, knit, timber and daisy props and the eight new native templates. Bowling-ball craters cut the shell and its inset core separately. Fabric, cork and foam interiors have separate finishes and carry through to the break debris. Collected salvage sells as Quartz through the existing converter and upgrade economy. This prototype activates only in preview on the authored quarry. The falling actors are runtime instances.

One native Play session from shared Edit, at place 124078027733625 and universe 10774898335, started with one worker and four junk objects. A normal bowling-ball remote produced one dent and a cream Fabric core in a separate client union. A normal clearing order broke the dented prop and selected another. A later batch included the suggestion box, gumball dispenser and alphabet block. Moving to the converter released the field claims, and Sell credited 25 Quartz for 25 collected units. ## Earlier follow-up — temporary mining tools and player damage

The second eligible drone-mined rock drops a **bowling ball**. Later breaks introduce the **crowbar** and **jelly grenade**, then vary the drop, with a six-break ceiling after the first. Drops hop out of rocks, last 75 seconds in the world, and are collected by their owner on contact into three hotbar slots with tool models and durability bars. Press **1–3** or select a slot; click to use it, use the touch action, or cycle with **LB** and use **RT** on a gamepad. The custom hotbar replaces the stock Roblox backpack interface. Existing Orbital Beacon and Scrap Capsule pockets and progression are retained. These field tools are temporary runtime gear; death, prestige and disconnect clear them without changing saved drone ownership.

- **Bowling ball:** the preview and swept server flight share a 420 studs/s² trajectory. Rock impact leaves a cavity at the contact face and throws short-lived rubble. The ball can be picked up after its first three throws, then shatters on impact after the fourth.
- **Jelly grenade:** sticks to the rock and follows its shrink; it detonates after 1.35 seconds. The explosion damages nearby eligible rocks and exposed players, including the thrower.
- **Crowbar:** twelve close-range strikes. A rejected hit does not spend durability.
- **Player contact:** ready rocks are solid and stop blocking immediately on collapse. Mining and combat beams, sentry fire, heavy impacts, and rubble can damage players. Cover, spawn shields and the depot protect players. All damage, durability, ownership, and mining rewards remain server-authoritative. Temporary tools cannot generate a drop loop or mine a locked rock or another player's reserved rock.

All gameplay drone types, display drones, company couriers, scrap bots, carriers and flying backpacks receive a clean dark silhouette outline on their actual client-rendered models. The permanent HUD now contains all five named mineral wallets (Quartz, Ruby, Cobalt, Violet and Emerald) and backpack space. Removed permanent Prestige, Shops, Fleet, Cards, Buy drones, next-objective and duplicate E-action panels, plus M/O menu shortcuts and clicking drones to open their roster remotely. Native prompts are the entry points: the existing Printer opens Drone yard with purchases, transport, storage, Fleet and Cards inside; Prestige has its own repaired prompt; each mineral shop opens only its own upgrades. Walking beyond a station closes its menus. The crate keeps its existing reveal/choice flow.

Locked-field rocks were explicitly hidden by the client; they are now rendered normally so the next minerals are visible goals. Unlock, ownership, line-of-sight and sentry rules remain server-enforced. Fixed station-distance mismatches: the printer's near side and far side both support purchases, and prestige validates the actual prestige station instead of also requiring the unrelated home radius.

## What caused the confusion

Earlier instructions named the historical place as the sole destination and treated the local file and cloud titles as interchangeable. The actual current `.rbxl` was excluded from Git, so another checkout could have Sources and historical captures without the real authored document. This handoff corrects that: one tracked native file, one shared repository, explicit IDs, separate publication evidence, and a short durable context for other Codex sessions. Older review notes describe the revisions they tested; they do not override the identities above.

## What the owner wants

- A simple Roblox game with a clear **buy/sell/upgrade/prestige** loop. The newest request supersedes the earlier temporary direction away from progression.
- Directing drones, breaking rocks and collecting should feel satisfying and Roblox-like. Keep original models, segmented pyramid turret, map identity, compact colorful card UI and accepted audio/VFX.
- Cubic rocks must snap smaller, retaining orientation. Currency must arc up and away from the rock before attraction, so it is visible.
- Only one drone type may mine a given rock. Do not restore complementary-worker dependencies or factory-management complexity.
- Rocks can drop useful stored consumables, including a placeable orbital laser. Avoid substituting generic timed multipliers for meaningful actions.
- Bee Swarm Simulator is a reference for enjoyable play, not permission to copy its assets or introduce every mechanic it has.
- Do not mistake earlier ore/reward timing or cosmetic tweaks for proof of an improved core loop. Automated correctness is not proof of fun.
- Preserve the initial Studio open state: leave it open if it was already open when work began; close it after work only if the agent opened it from a closed state. Check initial state without launching Studio. Always stop Play/Run and verify Edit mode before handing back; Studio may remain open, but no playtest or simulation may remain running. Routine stop/install/save/restart and publication to the existing DRONEREVOLUTION place remain authorized. New experiences need an explicit request.

## Current implementation

An unmarked copy of the original drone delivers each paid upgrade pack between the buyer and the pack shop. The server saves the tier and the three choices with the debit, withholds the choices until the owner completes the hold near the crate, and saves the opened state before showing the cards. A reconnect redelivers a sealed pack with its original tier and choices. Previously disclosed offers remain available. The shop interaction reaches 14 studs and the landed crate hold reaches 8 studs. Keyboard, gamepad and pointer/touch now use a standard Roblox ProximityPrompt. The nearby owner crate suppresses map prompts locally so the shop cannot consume E.

| Crate | Box odds | Hold | Color progression |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Common | 66% | 0.42 s | Grey |
| Rare | 24% | 1.32 s | Grey → yellow |
| Epic | 8% | 2.65 s | Grey → yellow → green |
| Legendary | 2% | 4.80 s | Grey → yellow → green → violet |

These timings, odds and the violet finish are tuning choices. One continuous bar fills quickly at first, then slows at each color boundary without resetting. A single native hold runs for the full rarity duration; the stock-circle restart workaround is superseded. Non-common boxes favor rarer existing cards among otherwise equally ranked offers. Cards rise from the crate when it is visible to the camera and retain the existing card effects and choice behavior. The reveal uses depth-of-field blur behind the cards.

Revision `88ba15d` connects the ordinary one-drone start to immediate selling, visible purchases and prestige:

- Converter Sell pays matching mineral wallets directly, without first filling the 120-unit launch storage.
- Mining power costs 20/40/80/160 Quartz and adds 50% base damage per level within the existing run cap.
- A permanent ordinary Miner starts at 60 Quartz and scales by 1.8 with owned Miners. Purchased drone IDs survive prestige.
- Prestige adds the existing earned drone and +25% permanent base mining power per prestige, clears run money/sales/modifiers, and retains permanent ownership.
- New Studio Play profiles start with one drone, 40 capacity and 100 Quartz for pack testing. Production profiles start at zero. Preview crews are explicit developer controls.
- Compact purchase/prestige guidance and the Buy drones action connect the UI. Two observed HUD overlaps were corrected.
- Stored Orbital/Scrap consumables, physical rock collapse interactions, snap-size cubic rocks, outward pickup arcs and one-type-per-rock behavior remain integrated.

Server owns damage, rewards, persistent state, cards, purchases and prestige. Clients render replicated intent. Preserve fail-safe persistence; a load failure must not overwrite a valid profile with defaults. See [core-loop report](review/CORE_LOOP.md), [consumables](review/CONSUMABLE_GAMEPLAY.md), [rock interactions](review/ROCK_INTERACTIONS.md), and [architecture](game/architecture.md).

## Pack Shop

The depot pack counter has its own Pack Shop sign and proximity prompt. Press E to order a crate directly; the prompt displays the price, currently FREE (0 Quartz). There is no pack purchase menu. Mineral shops continue to sell their level upgrades. The native refresh checked that all non-Source data and unrelated scripts matched the prior file. The delivery flow was played before merging the compact shop layout.

The five mineral shops now use compact upgrade tiles with a symbol, short name, effect, and one purchase or status button. Every catalog upgrade has a symbol, and field unlocks use their destination mineral color. Upgrade prices and transaction rules remain as before. Native Source preservation was verified offline. The updated shop layouts still need Studio visual and input verification.

## What was actually tested

The pack revision `eab05ef` completed a one-player Studio Play test before the shop merge. Normal mining and sale commands earned 40 Quartz, and the shop UI bought a Rare crate. Studio observed the crate grounded between shop and buyer. Releasing E early kept it sealed. A full hold advanced grey to yellow, disclosed the three cards, and showed all three card faces with world depth-of-field and transparent menu surfaces. Selecting Shard Harvester installed one card, cleared the pending offer and removed the crate and blur. The console was empty and Play was stopped. The canonical disk file was verified against the seven allowlisted Sources. The source-only refresh preserved map, other instances and backup Sources.

The core-loop suite passed in both configurations, including all four timing curves, sealed disclosure, cross-player rejection, interrupted holds, reconnect and exactly one claim. The native package/preservation suite passed. The legacy card price and HUD text assertions also fail on the baseline commit. Pack animation on touch, higher-tier visuals, listening quality, multiple Studio clients and production DataStore reconnects remain unverified. The reconciled release `ef812b5` was subsequently published as v14. Higher-tier native visual and multiplayer checks remain outstanding.

Production-logic checks covered sale transactions, purchase costs, ownership, prestige and preservation. The one-file packaging/preservation suite passed. Server/client bundles compiled.

An actual native single-player command-driven run used real mining, pickup, movement and normal command remotes: fill 40 → sell 40 → buy power for 20 → sell another 40 → buy a Miner for 60 → reach 160 lifetime sales → prestige. It retained both original owned drone IDs and added the third; run money and modifiers reset; permanent mining power increased. No money/cargo/damage/ownership grants were used. Console output was empty, Play was stopped, the canonical file was saved, and Studio was closed.

Limits: no final native multiplayer/late-join pass, real DataStore reconnect/server-restart validation, or owner enjoyment approval. The final expanded tool-pocket layout was compiled/installed but not separately played in Studio. See [native transaction observations](review/core-loop-native.json). Offline checks must never be reported as visual or multiplayer playtests.

## Next work

1. Add the friend's actual GitHub account as a collaborator when the owner supplies it. Use this shared repository.
2. For each completed game update, reconcile any newer shared-game edits, publish the canonical file to the existing DRONEREVOLUTION starting place, then record the actual published version/revision and verified destination here. Do not assume the instructions already completed this step.
3. Let the owner judge the simple loop in play. Prioritize meaningful mining and purchases over more decorative changes. Complete real multiplayer and durable-save checks before claiming those outcomes.
4. Keep Sources, the canonical native file and this handoff synchronized for every subsequent contribution. Never resolve a binary conflict by silently taking one contributor's map over another's.
